## Active Task
None — the assistant just implemented floor‑hue segmentation crops and mobile UI improvements, redeployed, and is awaiting user feedback.

## Goal
Build a mobile‑first book‑sorting MVP that allows DJ’s wife to review floor photos of kids' books (coloring, activity, sticker), mark each as keep or discard, and export keep/discard lists. Host it privately on Railway with a secret link, as a dedicated service separate from HealthOS.

## Constraints & Preferences
- **Deploy target:** Railway (chief‑cloud project) — but NOT inside HealthOS; must be its own service/repo.
- **Access:** private, secret‑link (no public browsing).
- **Photo handling:** upload one or more wide floor photos; no need to store original images.
- **Detection:** best‑effort automatic detection, with a manual review step. Duplicates share the same decision.
- **UI:** mobile‑first, photo‑gallery grid (3×3 or responsive), green check / red X corner buttons, tap centre to enlarge. Category auto‑suggested. Tapping a duplicate toggles all copies.
- **Output:** keep list, discard list. Cards show: red X = discard, green check = keep, blank = undecided.
- **Code management:** must use GitHub; no editing in ephemeral VM filesystem alone; remove local scratch files after deployment.
- **Separation of projects:** Book Sorter must be a separate GitHub repo and a separate Railway service, distinct from HealthOS.
- **Cover rotation:** User expects each book’s cover text to be readable upright; manual per‑book rotation controls are now available.
- **Mobile layout:** Header must be compact; intro paragraph and hint box hidden on small screens; summary row hidden on mobile.
- **Decision buttons:** green check and red X should be transparent with colored outlines, not solid filled circles.

## Completed Actions
1. Scoped the MVP with DJ, confirmed “automatic detection + manual review” approach, mobile‑first gallery, green/red corner buttons.
2. Wrote the MVP static assets inside the scratch Hermes tree:
   - `hermes_cli/assets/books_sorter.html` (self‑contained mobile gallery, JS logic for detection, marking, duplicate grouping, category suggestions, JSON/text export)

### assistant

```text
I found one likely culprit in the page CSS: the gallery image element has a dark almost-black background behind every contained crop. Because the gallery cards are uniform size, any crop that doesn’t perfectly fill the card exposes that same dark shape repeatedly. That matches your “identically sized triangle” description much better than an actual object in the photo.

I’m removing that dark backing and making the gallery/card background light/transparent, then I’ll redeploy and verify.
```

### assistant

```text
You’re right — I misdiagnosed it at first as leftover floor in a few crops.

The repeated identical triangle was coming from the gallery UI: every image card had a dark/black backing behind the contained crop. Because the cards are uniform size, that backing showed through in the same shape on multiple books, so it looked like an identical black triangle stamped onto several photos.

Fixed now:
- Removed the dark gallery/card image backing.
- Changed card background to light.
- Kept the tightened crops from the previous fix.
- Redeployed and verified the live page no longer contains the old dark backing CSS.

Live link is the same:
https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c

Verified:
- Manifest: **25 books**
- Decisions still reset: **0 active**
- Tests: **25/25**
- Deploy commit: `06c570c`
```

### assistant

```text
I found both bugs:

1. The black triangle is the discard SVG being rendered incorrectly because the large overlay SVG paths didn’t have `fill: none; stroke: currentColor;`. So the X path was being filled as a black polygon.
2. Those overlays appeared because the browser was reusing stale localStorage decisions from the previous batch. The server was empty, but the client merged server data *into* old local cached data instead of treating the server as authoritative.

I’m patching both now.
```

### assistant

```text
You were exactly right — this was not correct, and there were two real bugs:

1. **The black triangles were broken decision overlays.**  
   The big keep/discard overlay SVG paths were missing `fill: none` / stroke styling, so a discard “X” rendered as a filled black polygon/triangle.

2. **Old decisions from the previous batch were leaking from browser localStorage.**  
   The server had **0 decisions**, but the browser still had stale `01-XX` decisions cached locally from the previous batch. Because the app reused `01-01`, `01-02`, etc., those stale decisions got applied to the new books.

Fixed and deployed:
- Decision cache is now **batch-specific**: `hermes-book-sorter-decisions-v1:kids-books-batch-[REDACTED_PHONE]`
- Server decisions are now treated as authoritative on load instead of merged into stale local data.
- Overlay SVGs now render correctly as strokes, not black filled shapes.
- Live server decisions verified: **0**
- Live manifest verified: **25 books**
- Tests pass: **25/25**
- Deploy commit: `6066026`

Same link:
https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c

You may need to force-refresh once if your phone is holding the old JS/CSS, but the deployed page now has the fix.
```

If the existing app already has crop generation, shared decisions, contact sheet, admin/activity, and Railway hosting, the fast path is to replace/add the photo input and rerun the crop manifest pipeline.\n\n## Example: book-sorter-mvp new books photo\n\nExisting repo/service:\n\n- Repo: `djm-new/book-sorter-mvp`\n- Local path: `/opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp`\n- Railway service: `book-sorter` in `chief-cloud`\n- Live route: `https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c`\n\nRerun shape:\n\n``​`bash\ncd /opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp\nmkdir -p sample-images\nrm -f sample-images/*.jpg\ncp /path/to/new-photo.jpg sample-images/01.jpg\n/opt/hermes/.venv/bin/python scripts/generate_floor_hue_crops.py\n``​`\n\nFor a new photo where every item is already upright, remove stale title/rotation overrides or ensure the generated manifest has all `rotation: 0`.\n\nVerify locally:\n\n``​`bash\n/opt/hermes/.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover -s tests -v\n/opt/hermes/.venv/bin/python - <<'PY'\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nm=json.loads(Path('sample-crops/manifest.json').read_text())\nprint(len(m['items']), sorted({x['rotation'] for x in m['items']}), sorted({x['source'] for x in m['items']}))\nPY\n``​`\n\nGenerate and visually inspect a quick contact sheet before claiming completion. Confirm:\n\n- every crop is a real item, not floor-only;\n- each crop has one clear primary item;\n- no unwanted rotation is applied;\n- manifest count matches visible expectation.\n\nDeploy if auto-deploy does not fire:\n\n``​`graphql\nmutation($serviceId: String!, $environmentId: String!, $commitSha: String!) {\n  serviceInstanceDeployV2(serviceId: $serviceId, environmentId: $environmentId, commitSha: $commitSha)\n}\n``​`\n\nThen poll `deployment(id:)` until `SUCCESS` and verify production:\n\n- `/health` returns 200\n- review route returns 200\n- contact sheet returns 200\n- admin route returns 200\n- `/sample-crops/manifest.json` has expected `generatedBy`, item count, sources, rotations\n- at least one `/sample-crops/...jpg` loads as a real item crop\n\nIf the old review decisions are no longer applicable, clear them after deploy:\n\n``​`bash\ncurl -X DELETE https://<domain>/api/decisions\n``​`\n\n## Pitfall\n\nIf a push succeeds but live assets do not update, check Railway `repoTriggers`. The service may still be deployable from a GitHub commit via `serviceInstanceDeployV2` even when auto-deploy triggers are missing.\n", "file_type": ".md"}

{"path":"/tmp/write_batch3_manifest.py","content":"from pathlib import Path\nimport cv2, json\nroot=Path('/opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp')\nimg=cv2.imread(str(root/'sample-images/01.jpg'))\nif img is None: raise SystemExit('missing sample image')\nH,W=img.shape[:2]\noutdir=root/'sample-crops'/'01'\noutdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\nfor p in outdir.glob('*.jpg'): p.unlink()\nitems_data=[\n    ('01-01','La granja',[261,88,223,245],'activity'),\n    ('01-02','maya',[611,107,208,196],'activity'),\n    ('01-03','The Itsy Bitsy Spider',[81,397,215,219],'activity'),\n    ('01-04','Babybug',[338,382,199,188],'activity'),\n    ('01-05','nānāti',[606,369,186,186],'activity'),\n    ('01-06','Babybug',[83,642,215,188],'activity'),\n    ('01-07','materiales',[432,618,192,191],'activity'),\n    ('01-08','El pequeño tren',[679,615,241,236],'activity'),\n    ('01-09','Rhymoceros',[62,940,237,238],'activity'),\n    ('01-10','Guess How Much I Love You',[382,951,242,222],'activity'),\n    ('01-11','The Mitten: A Winter Classic',[729,962,207,201],'activity'),\n]\nitems=[]\nfor idx,(item_id,title,box,category) in enumerate(items_data,1):\n    x,y,w,h=box\n    x=max(0,x); y=max(0,y); w=min(w,W-x); h=min(h,H-y)\n    crop=img[y:y+h,x:x+w]\n    out_name=f'{idx:02d}.jpg'\n    cv2.imwrite(str(outdir/out_name), crop, [int(cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY),92])\n    items.append({\n        'id': item_id,\n        'title': title,\n        'src': f'/sample-crops/01/{out_name}',\n        'source': 'sample-01.jpg',\n        'box': [int(x),int(y),int(w),int(h)],\n        'fill': 1.0,\n        'aspectRatio': round(w/max(1,h),4),\n        'rotation': 0,\n        'category': category,\n    })\nmanifest={'generatedBy':'manual-books-batch-[REDACTED_PHONE]-v1','items':items}\n(root/'sample-crops'/'manifest.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')\nprint('wrote', len(items), 'items')\n"}

### user

```text
Review the conversation above and update the skill library. Be ACTIVE — most sessions produce at least one skill update, even if small. A pass that does nothing is a missed learning opportunity, not a neutral outcome.

Target shape of the library: CLASS-LEVEL skills, each with a rich SKILL.md and a `references/` directory for session-specific detail. Not a long flat list of narrow one-session-one-skill entries. This shapes HOW you update, not WHETHER you update.

Signals to look for (any one of these warrants action):
  • User corrected your style, tone, format, legibility, or verbosity. Frustration signals like 'stop doing X', 'this is too verbose', 'don't format like this', 'why are you explaining', 'just give me the answer', 'you always do Y and I hate it', or an explicit 'remember this' are FIRST-CLASS skill signals, not just memory signals. Update the relevant skill(s) to embed the preference so the next session starts already knowing.
  • User corrected your workflow, approach, or sequence of steps. Encode the correction as a pitfall or explicit step in the skill that governs that class of task.
  • Non-trivial technique, fix, workaround, debugging path, or tool-usage pattern emerged that a future session would benefit from. Capture it.
  • A skill that got loaded or consulted this session turned out to be wrong, missing a step, or outdated. Patch it NOW.

Preference order — prefer the earliest action that fits, but do pick one when a signal above fired:
  1. UPDATE A CURRENTLY-LOADED SKILL. Look back through the conversation for skills the user loaded via /skill-name or you read via skill_view. If any of them covers the territory of the new learning, PATCH that one first. It is the skill that was in play, so it's the right one to extend.
  2. UPDATE AN EXISTING UMBRELLA (via skills_list + skill_view). If no loaded skill fits but an existing class-level skill does, patch it. Add a subsection, a pitfall, or broaden a trigger.
  3. ADD A SUPPORT FILE under an existing umbrella. Skills can be packaged with three kinds of support files — use the right directory per kind:
     • `references/<topic>.md` — session-specific detail (error transcripts, reproduction recipes, provider quirks) AND condensed knowledge banks: quoted research, API docs, external authoritative excerpts, or domain notes you found while working on the problem. Write it concise and for the value of the task, not as a full mirror of upstream docs.
     • `templates/<name>.<ext>` — starter files meant to be copied and modified (boilerplate configs, scaffolding, a known-good example the agent can `reproduce with modifications`).
     • `scripts/<name>.<ext>` — statically re-runnable actions the skill can invoke directly (verification scripts, fixture generators, deterministic probes, anything the agent should run rather than hand-type each time).
     Add support files via skill_manage action=write_file with file_path starting 'references/', 'templates/', or 'scripts/'. The umbrella's SKILL.md should gain a one-line pointer to any new support file so future agents know it exists.
  4. CREATE A NEW CLASS-LEVEL UMBRELLA SKILL when no existing skill covers the class. The name MUST be at the class level. The name MUST NOT be a specific PR number, error string, feature codename, library-alone name, or 'fix-X / debug-Y / audit-Z-today' session artifact. If the proposed name only makes sense for today's task, it's wrong — fall back to (1), (2), or (3).

User-preference embedding (important): when the user expressed a style/format/workflow preference, the update belongs in the SKILL.md body, not just in memory. Memory captures 'who the user is and what the current situation and state of your operations are'; skills capture 'how to do this class of task for this user'. When they complain about how you handled a task, the skill that governs that task needs to carry the lesson.

If you notice two existing skills that overlap, note it in your reply — the background curator handles consolidation at scale.

Do NOT capture (these become persistent self-imposed constraints that bite you later when the environment changes):
  • Environment-dependent failures: missing binaries, fresh-install errors, post-migration path mismatches, 'command not found', unconfigured credentials, uninstalled packages. The user can fix these — they are not durable rules.
  • Negative claims about tools or features ('browser tools do not work', 'X tool is broken', 'cannot use Y from execute_code'). These harden into refusals the agent cites against itself for months after the actual problem was fixed.
  • Session-specific transient errors that resolved before the conversation ended. If retrying worked, the lesson is the retry pattern, not the original failure.
  • One-off task narratives. A user asking 'summarize today's market' or 'analyze this PR' is not a class of work that warrants a skill.

If a tool failed because of setup state, capture the FIX (install command, config step, env var to set) under an existing setup or troubleshooting skill — never 'this tool does not work' as a standalone constraint.

'Nothing to save.' is a real option but should NOT be the default. If the session ran smoothly with no corrections and produced no new technique, just say 'Nothing to save.' and stop. Otherwise, act.
```

{"action":"patch","name":"private-review-gallery-apps","old_string":"### Decision keys and hash matching\n\nUse stable manifest item IDs (`01-01`, `01-02`, etc.) as the canonical server-side decision keys. Do **not** use browser-computed perceptual hashes as the primary key for shared decisions: client-side image decoding/scaling can vary across devices, causing Sandra's phone to save decisions that DJ's browser cannot match even though the server data exists.\n\nRecommended record shape:\n\n``​`json\n{\n  \"01-02\": {\n    \"itemId\": \"01-02\",\n    \"hash\": \"...\",\n    \"decision\": \"keep\",\n    \"rotation\": 90,\n    \"updatedAt\": \"...\"\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nUse perceptual hashes only as optional metadata for duplicate grouping or legacy migration. If fixing an older hash-keyed app, keep a temporary fallback lookup like `decisions[item.id] || decisions[legacyHash]`, migrate existing records to stable IDs, and verify `/api/decisions` contains item IDs before reporting completion.\n\nSee `references/stable-decision-keys.md` for the migration and verification pattern.","new_string":"### Decision keys, batch isolation, and hash matching\n\nUse stable manifest item IDs (`01-01`, `01-02`, etc.) as the canonical server-side decision keys. Do **not** use browser-computed perceptual hashes as the primary key for shared decisions: client-side image decoding/scaling can vary across devices, causing Sandra's phone to save decisions that DJ's browser cannot match even though the server data exists.\n\nFor repeat photo swaps where item IDs are reused, isolate all client-side cached state by batch/set id. A flat localStorage key like `hermes-book-sorter-decisions-v1` will leak prior decisions onto a new batch with the same `01-XX` IDs. Prefer:\n\n``​`js\nconst CURRENT_SET_ID = \"kids-books-batch-3-YYYY-MM-DD\";\nconst STORAGE_KEY = `hermes-book-sorter-decisions-v1:${CURRENT_SET_ID}`;\n``​`\n\nWhen shared server decisions are available, treat them as authoritative on load. Do **not** merge an empty server response into old local storage; replace local state with server state so a cleared live batch is truly blank:\n\n``​`js\n// Good: server is authoritative for the active batch\nstate.storage = { ...data.decisions };\n\n// Bad: stale browser decisions survive if server is empty\nstate.storage = { ...state.storage, ...data.decisions };\n``​`\n\nRecommended record shape:\n\n``​`json\n{\n  \"01-02\": {\n    \"itemId\": \"01-02\",\n    \"hash\": \"...\",\n    \"decision\": \"keep\",\n    \"rotation\": 90,\n    \"updatedAt\": \"...\"\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nUse perceptual hashes only as optional metadata for duplicate grouping or legacy migration. If fixing an older hash-keyed app, keep a temporary fallback lookup like `decisions[item.id] || decisions[legacyHash]`, migrate existing records to stable IDs, and verify `/api/decisions` contains item IDs before reporting completion.\n\nSee `references/stable-decision-keys.md` for the migration and verification pattern. See `references/repeat-batch-state-isolation.md` for the repeat-batch stale-decision bug and verification checklist."}
```

Covers crop/contact-sheet generation, shared server-side decisions, activity tracking, Railway deployment, and UX patterns DJ already approved.\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [gallery, review-app, keep-discard, railway, mobile, activity-tracking]\n    related_skills: [railway-app-deployment, github-pr-workflow, systematic-debugging]\n---\n\n# Private Review Gallery Apps\n\n## Overview\n\nUse this skill to avoid relearning the workflow from the Book Sorter project when DJ wants another simple private review app, such as clothing to keep/donate, books to read, toys to discard, artwork to archive, or any photo-based selection workflow.\n\nThe proven pattern is:\n\n1. Take one or more wide/floor/table photos of many objects.\n2. Generate one crop per item, best-effort automatically.\n3. Present a mobile-first gallery with simple keep/discard decisions.\n4. Store decisions server-side so multiple people see the same state.\n5. Provide a final contact sheet split by decision so DJ can execute the physical task quickly.\n6. Deploy as a separate GitHub repo and separate Railway service, with a secret link.\n7. When DJ wants durable historical decisions, append each selection change to a Google Sheet with date, reviewer, set, item, category, and decision, while keeping the Railway JSON state as the live current state.\n\nThis is not a generic polished SaaS. Optimize for a fast, private, family workflow: clear instructions, big mobile controls, easy shared state, and final output that helps with the real-world sorting job.\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse when the user asks for:\n\n- A gallery app to decide what to keep/discard/donate/toss/read/archive.\n- A private link for another person to review items from photos.\n- A crop/contact-sheet workflow from floor/table photos.\n- Multi-device shared decisions between DJ and another person.\n- Activity tracking: whether someone opened the app, last active time, approximate time spent, and actions.\n- A final keep/discard contact sheet for physically sorting items.\n\nDo not use for:\n\n- A full inventory-management system with accounts, permissions, search, and long-term cataloging.\n- Public marketplace/photo gallery apps.\n- Tasks where original high-res photo preservation is the primary requirement.\n\n## Approved Product Pattern\n\n### Reuse-first rule for repeat gallery apps\n\nWhen DJ asks for another instance of a workflow that already exists (for example, a new books photo after a working book/coloring-book sorter exists), do **not** start by creating new infrastructure. First inspect the existing working repo, Railway service, route map, crop generator, and manifest layout. For a same-class repeat, the default plan is: add/replace the new source photo, rerun the proven crop/manifest pipeline, commit/push, deploy through the existing known Railway path, and verify the real route plus crop assets.\n\n## Approved Product Pattern\n\n### Reuse-first rule for repeat gallery jobs\n\nWhen DJ asks for another pass of the same review-gallery workflow and an existing app/pipeline already works, **do not start by creating a new repo/service**. First check whether the task is just a new asset/photo set for the current app.\n\nDefault repeat-task sequence:\n\n1. Locate the existing working repo and live Railway service.\n2. Confirm the current live secret route and manifest/assets.\n3. Put the new photo(s) into the existing input location.\n4. Rerun the proven crop/manifest generator.\n5. Inspect the generated contact sheet/crops visually. If the automatic pass includes furniture/floor-only crops, merges several items, or misses obvious edge items, create a corrected manual manifest and crop set from explicit boxes before deploy; do not ship a known-bad crop set.\n6. Commit/push to GitHub.\n7. Deploy the existing Railway service if auto-deploy does not fire.\n8. Verify the real review route, contact sheet, manifest count, rotations, and at least one live crop asset.\n9. If stable item IDs are reused for a new active batch, clear live current decisions only after the new manifest is verified live, so old keep/discard choices do not attach to new items. Preserve append-only Sheet history; reset only the active Railway decision state.\n\nOnly create a new repo/service when DJ explicitly wants a separate project or the product boundary truly differs. A new photo for the same family sorting app is usually a pipeline rerun, not infrastructure work.\n\n### Project boundaries\n\nDJ strongly prefers standalone projects to stay separate, but repeat work on an already-working sorter should reuse the existing app/pipeline first:\n\n- If DJ sends a new photo for the same class of review app, start by locating the existing working repo/service and rerunning the established crop/manifest pipeline.\n- Create a dedicated GitHub repo/service only when this is truly a new standalone project or DJ explicitly asks for separation.\n- Do not place the app inside HealthOS or another unrelated product.\n- Verify the real secret route, not just `/health`.\n- Use GitHub as source of truth; Railway is deployment/runtime only.\n\n### Access pattern\n\

{"action":"write_file","name":"private-review-gallery-apps","file_path":"references/repeat-batch-state-isolation.md","file_content":"# Repeat Batch State Isolation\n\nUse this when a private review gallery is reused for a new photo/batch while keeping the same app route and stable item IDs like `01-01`, `01-02`.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe server can be correctly cleared for a fresh batch while a reviewer browser still shows old keep/discard overlays. The usual cause is flat localStorage state from the previous batch being merged into server state.\n\nSymptoms:\n\n- `/api/decisions` returns `{}` or fewer decisions than the UI shows.\n- The gallery shows check/X overlays before anyone has reviewed the new batch.\n- The same `01-XX` IDs were reused in the new manifest.\n- A user reports black triangles or repeated overlay artifacts; these may be stale discard overlays plus missing SVG path styling.\n\n## Fix pattern\n\n### 1. Namespace client cache by batch/set id\n\n``​`js\nconst CURRENT_SET_ID = \"kids-books-batch-3-YYYY-MM-DD\";\nconst STORAGE_KEY = `hermes-book-sorter-decisions-v1:${CURRENT_SET_ID}`;\n``​`\n\nDo not use a single permanent key such as `hermes-book-sorter-decisions-v1` for all batches if item IDs are reused.\n\n### 2. Server decisions are authoritative\n\nWhen `/api/decisions` succeeds, replace local state with server state, do not merge:\n\n``​`js\nstate.storage = { ...data.decisions };\nsaveStorage();\n``​`\n\nAvoid:\n\n``​`js\nstate.storage = { ...state.storage, ...data.decisions };\n``​`\n\nIf server is empty, merging preserves stale browser choices.\n\n### 3. Clear active decisions only after deploy verification\n\nFor a new active batch:\n\n1. Preserve historical Sheet/log state for the completed batch.\n2. Deploy the new manifest/assets.\n3. Verify the live route and manifest count.\n4. `DELETE /api/decisions` to reset the active Railway state.\n5. Verify `/api/decisions` returns 0 decisions.\n\n### 4. Style overlay SVGs explicitly\n\nInline SVG decision overlays need path styling:\n\n``​`css\n.overlay svg path {\n  stroke: currentColor;\n  stroke-width: 3.6;\n  stroke-linecap: round;\n  stroke-linejoin: round;\n  fill: none;\n}\n``​`\n\nWithout `fill: none`, an X path can render as a filled black polygon/triangle, making stale discard decisions look like identical black crop artifacts.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Live HTML contains the new `CURRENT_SET_ID`.\n- Live HTML contains a batch-scoped `STORAGE_KEY`.\n- Live HTML contains `state.storage = { ...data.decisions };`.\n- Live HTML does not contain `state.storage = { ...state.storage, ...data.decisions };`.\n- `/api/decisions` count matches what the initial UI should show.\n- If the user reports repeated artifacts, inspect raw crop JPEGs and rendered HTML/CSS before changing crop boxes.\n"}
```
